So in","contrast to a daily tidal model with relatively clean noise-free data,","we are confronted with noisier data that needs to extend for decades to","generate a precise prediction.","","Given that, we can try a high resolution fit for the historical training","period of the interval 1880 to 2010 and see how well it works beyond","this point, as shown in \\textbf{FIGURE\\_12-24}. The base training is","from 1880 to 2010 (see upper panel \\textbf{a}), and further refined","training is performed on the interval from 1980 to 2010 (see middle","panel \\textbf{b}). This refined training should weight the fit to better","align to the data closer to the years of the projection. The projection","readily identifies the El Nino peaks of 2010-2011 and 2016-2017. To see","the limitation of the fitting process, we can compare the correlation of","SOI to another metric for ENSO, NINO3.4, which is plotted in lower panel","\\textbf{c}. Note a very high sliding correlation coefficient over the","1980 to 2010 interval, yet still good agreement elsewhere. The","interpretation is that the underlying pattern represents poorer","coefficients in intervals where the noise in the data starts to encroach","on the ENSO signal.","","The other option for cross-validation is to attempt a back-fit to the","ENSO signal derived from historical proxy data (mainly from corals)","{[}48{]}. The Unified ENSO Proxy (UEP) data set extends back to 1650 and","is calibrated to the modern instrumental measurements such as SOI.","","\\textbf{FIGURE\\_12-25} provides a check of the ENSO model by","back-extrapolating against the UEP records. The UE proxy is averaged","together from various data sets and only has a resolution of one year,","with a distinct possibility that the absolute yearly alignment could be","off. The alignment is easy to determine for the modern instrumental era","as one can calibrate against SOI and NINO data, but for coral proxy data","before 1880, the calibration becomes more difficult to verify. This is","important because a tidal forcing model is critically dependent on","keeping absolute orbital phase relationships over all times. The UEP","goes back to 1650, providing a the time series about 330 years long, so","still not quite long enough to detect the 372 year base repeat pattern","in the ENSO behavior.","","With that said, the pre-1880 ENSO behavior can be captured effectively","by slightly relaxing the fit to the SOI data that the model was","initially based on. The top panel \\textbf{a} shows a fit to the yearly","UEP data which continues through to the modern SOI-based ENSO record","shown in panel \\textbf{b}. One complicating here is that the UEP records","are yearly while the SOI records are monthly, so that a monthly fit was","produced for years prior to 1880 and the result simply averaged over a","12-month window.","","So with this particular fit, the temporal lunisolar forcing pattern was","unchanged before and after 1880, but a slight temporal shift (at least","partly due to the yearly to month transition) and an independent spatial","scaling (impacting the amplitude) was applied prior to 1880. Recall that","only three lunar periods are involved in the fit, along with the annual","harmonics. From 1650 to 1880 the correlation coefficient is above 0.80.","","Before \\textasciitilde1900, the variance of the UEP appears to be","significantly reduced as described in {[}48{]}. This indicates the","possibility that the standing wave boundary conditions changed around","that time (perhaps due to the Krakatoa volcanic eruption of 1883 which","ostensibly impacted the Indonesian outflow connecting the Pacific and","Indian oceans {[}71{]}, {[}72{]}), or that some other long-term","variation is applicable. The stationary model we devised for ENSO has a","more stable variance over any particular 300 year time span than found","from the coral proxy data, so we are forced to parameterize the model","into two separate intervals. Yet, even with this adjustment, the ability","of a constant tidal forcing model consisting of only 3 lunar cycles and","1 annual cycle to fit both a 1650-1880 proxy time series and a","1880-current instrumental time series is remarkable, and thus shows","promise for very long-term predictions. Together with the","cross-validation results of Figure 12-24, where the potential for","short-term predictions is demonstrated, the ENSO model warrants further","investigation and close monitoring in the coming years.","","\\hypertarget{list-of-figure-captions}{%","\\section*{List of Figure Captions}\\label{list-of-figure-captions}}","\\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{List of Figure Captions}","","\\textbf{FIGURE\\_12-1}:","\\href{../figures/FIGURE_12-1.png}{} Tidal","analysis fitted to the training interval highlighted in yellow, with","prediction to the right","","\\textbf{FIGURE\\_12-2}:","\\href{../figures/FIGURE_12-2.png}{} Tidal","analysis power spectra of 12-1","","\\textbf{FIGURE\\_12-3}:","\\href{../figures/FIGURE_12-3.png}{}","Standing wave spatial wave-numbers superimposed on equatorial ENSO","temperature variations. The long-period modulation corresponds to the","Darwin-Tahiti dipole measured by SOI. The labelled standing Kelvin waves","may be more closely matched to tropical instability waves, which are","associated with ENSO {[}73{]}.","","\\textbf{FIGURE\\_12-4}:","\\href{../figures/FIGURE_12-4.png}{} ENSO","model trained over the SOI interval 1900-1920 using only a","biennially-modulated lunisolar tidal forcing.","","\\textbf{FIGURE\\_12-5}:","\\href{../figures/FIGURE_12-5.png}{} ENSO","model trained over the entire SOI time-series using only a","biennially-modulated lunisolar tidal forcing.","","\\textbf{FIGURE\\_12-6}:","\\href{../figures/FIGURE_12-6.png}{} (a)","Variation in the anomalistic month period. (b) Variation in the draconic","month period","","\\textbf{FIGURE\\_12-7}:","\\href{../figures/FIGURE_12-7.png}{} Power","spectra of ENSO data against model","","\\textbf{FIGURE\\_12-8}:","\\href{../figures/FIGURE_12-8.png}{}","Possible noisy intervals are indicated by poor sliding correlations","between SOI and NINO34. These agree with poor model correlations with","SOI.","","\\textbf{FIGURE\\_12-9}:","\\href{../figures/FIGURE_12-9.png}{}","Slightly varying the known Draconic and Anomalistic periods from their","known values results in a rapidly degrading correlation.","","\\textbf{FIGURE\\_12-10}:","\\href{../figures/FIGURE_12-10.png}{} The","aliasing of lunar months to calendar year requires the variations of","each to linearly scale in a dependent fashion.","","\\textbf{FIGURE\\_12-11}:","\\href{../figures/FIGURE_12-11.png}{} (a, b)","The goal is to model the spectral density of waves. Enough disorder","exists in open water that periodic coherence between spatially separate","waves is not maintained, measurement from wave buoy in north","Atlantic.(from {[}54{]}). (c) The initial assumption is that waves","contain energy proportional to their width and height. This","proportionality scales independent of volume. Total energy in a directed","wave goes as the square of the height, and the macroscopic fluid","properties suggest that it scales to size. This leads to a dispersive","form for the wave size distribution.","","\\textbf{FIGURE\\_12-12}:","\\href{../figures/FIGURE_12-12.png}{} The","CDIP data source provided archival wave energy statistics for assessing","models.","","\\textbf{FIGURE\\_12-13}:","\\href{../figures/FIGURE_12-13.png}{} Wave","energy spectra from two sites (a) and (b) off of the San Diego coastal","region. The Maximum Entropy estimate is in open circles.","","\\textbf{FIGURE\\_12-14}:","\\href{../figures/FIGURE_12-14.png}{} Highly","ordered waves with all harmonics generated in a wave tank. This is in","terms of temporal frequency and the spatial frequency can be recovered","via the dispersion relation.","","\\textbf{FIGURE\\_12-15}:","\\href{../figures/FIGURE_12-15.png}{} A PSD","from a wave tank showing ripples","","\\textbf{FIGURE\\_12-16}:","\\href{../figures/FIGURE_12-16.png}{} A","simulated wave which emulates crest-focused dynamics shows intermediate","order","","\\textbf{FIGURE\\_12-17}:","\\href{../figures/FIGURE_12-17.png}{} Total","energy in a directed wave goes as the square of the height, and the","macroscopic fluid properties suggest that it scales to size.
